I’m not usually like that, so I thought I should talk to someone before it becomes a bigger issue.\n\nDoctor: Has this kind of reaction—snapping or losing control—happened in other situations, or was this the first time?\n\nPatient: As far as I can tell, that board call was the first real instance where I actually snapped like that. It was just a small question about the facility budget, and I reacted too sharply. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was, but it bothered me because that’s not how I run things or how I treat people.\n\nDoctor: Since that incident on the board call, have you noticed yourself becoming more irritable or having difficulty managing your emotions in other situations?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s been happening all over the place, no. The main thing is still that board call… I snapped over a small question about the facility budget, and that’s what bothered me. It felt out of character for me. I try to run my company with more respect than that, so even if it was just one moment, it didn’t sit right with me.\n\nDoctor: In the time leading up to that board call, have you noticed any changes in your mood, like feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m hopeless or anything like that, no. It’s more… things that usually help settle me just aren’t landing the same. A chess problem, going over my hiking notes, even dinner with my wife — I know those are things I normally enjoy, and I should feel something from them, but right now they just feel kind of flat.\n\nI don’t mean to overstate it. It’s just noticeable enough that I’ve wondered if something’s off.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s like a bad film I didn’t ask to see. It passes, but it does catch me off guard.\n\nDoctor: Since the explosion and fire during the walkthrough, have you found yourself avoiding certain places, people, or activities that remind you of the event?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Since the explosion and fire, have you found yourself being more on edge or easily startled by things that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: Yes… some.
